We will need this offer, and the expansion will be necessary.

These are sustainable, structural, longer-term improvements. Ladies and gentlemen! Today is an important day for the future of care, a good day for the future of care, because it creates the basis for better reward for care professions in the future – with the value they actually bring to society and people in that country. I ask for your consent. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e12215	Madam President. The Minister is not in the hall right now. What is the function of statutory pension insurance? – This is the function of ensuring your income for old age. If you have earned a lot and paid a lot of contributions, you will receive a high pension if you have made little money and have paid little contributions, you will get a low pension – that is the insurance principle. And it is the good right of pensioners in Austria that they have an inflation insurance which is in the law, because no one has to do anything at all. This is carried out annually, always with 1. January. In this case, one can see how inflation was between August 2021 and July 2022, and that's what it is with 1. January 2023 on it. The government has also said – also with our agreement – that: Until then, we'll make a one-off payment. You don't have to be a mathematician to say: ==References== January 2023, under the law – for all who want to read it: § 108f ASVG – there will be a high pension increase. So now you don't have to open a barrel, that's safe, and that's good - on a very high level, really on a very high level. I would just like to say: Austrian average pensions are 60 percent higher than German average pensions, and this is already a significant level. Good social policy has a task and a responsibility, and this task is to maintain the balance between the interests of the beneficiaries and the interests of the contributors. The contributors are the workers who have a wage increase only once a year in accordance with the collective agreement and it is not fair to impose additional burdens on those employed and to postpone the balance. These workers also have difficult times behind themselves, corona crisis, short-time work, job loss, sales declines, and even now difficult times because they too are faced with higher costs. In the sense of this balance, such populist applications, 6 percent increases are preferable and similar, although beautiful in sale, the SPÖ gets nice headlines and certainly also receives friendly emails from pensioners, but this is not responsible and not fair, because we here do not have the responsibility for a particular group of persons, but for society as a whole, and there we need this balance. [[Congratulations with the NEOS.]] Let's see how much we spend on pensions! I now take the no longer up-to-date figures of the Federal Financial Framework: In this year, it is 23 billion euros, next year 24.8 billion euros and next year 26 billion euros, a sharp trend. The share of the Federal Budget, which is used up for pension subsidies, is increasing steadily. And we know from the social security plan that the values I have just read are too low every year by 1.5 billion euros. So it's not the time for populist gifts, because that's all someone has to pay – and that's the job and the pensioners of tomorrow and the day after, which we have to look at with the same right as those who are now in retirement.

Point Report of the Committee on Labour and Social Affairs on the Government Template (1523, e.g._Other Organiser Agreement between the Republic of Austria and the Federal Republic of Brazil on social security (1625, e.g.)
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e12303	We're on the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e12333	Madam President. Estimated Minister! This item is about an agreement between Austria and Brazil on social security. In recent years, some countries, particularly in Europe, have signed an agreement with Brazil, including Belgium, France, Germany and Luxembourg. There are also more and more Austrian companies that are based in Brazil, so the reputation from Austria has become louder to conclude an agreement with Brazil. Already in 2015, there were discussions involving experts, and in May 2016 an agreement was reached. However, the signature was a little delayed because in both countries, i.e. in Brazil and in Austria, government reforms were constantly taking place. At last, however, we are about to sign an agreement with the country of Brazil. What is the point of this agreement? – This bilateral international contract regulates pension insurance and cross-border employment. Through the agreement between Austria and Brazil, social insurance companies in both countries have the same or similar entitlements to social security benefits from these countries. The main benefits of this scheme are those who work across borders in both Contracting States. The agreement preserves its social rights. There are also agreements on pension insurance. If one has been collected in a country for too few years to be entitled to a pension, then future insured insurance years will be counted together for the pension claim. Agreements such as these allow us to work in a wide range of countries around the world, while ensuring social security and, of course, collecting pension years. Thank you.

It concludes the question: The debate on a future with less animal suffering and a paradigm change in farm animal husbandry begins. These discussions and negotiations will not be easy, but they will be conducted with a wide range of links between the industry and the NGOs, and they are a sign of a progressive change in diet, which is wanted and demanded by more and more people. Full column floors now have an expiry date. In 2039, we will no longer talk about how many pig farms need to be converted quickly to get out of the full-column system in time for 2040. In 2039, the world, but also our agricultural system, will look different. The consumption of meat, as it is common today, will no longer be possible – the End of Meat – because we are not viable without the paradigm change on the issue of nutrition, because the young generations and many of the old know it and because it is now no longer just a matter of climate, health or animal welfare, but also a matter of supply, food safety and the future. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] In 20 years, the world will look different, with significantly less farming animal husbandry and more and more demand for sustainable and vegetable food. Today we are setting the course for significant changes and will not only work for the best possible new standard in conventional pig farming, but also for the reduction of consumption of animal products and for the development and promotion of plant alternatives. The animal welfare package contains many important improvements, but there is one that I would like to highlight in particular: We were able to reach an extension of the infrastructure and the rights of the animal welfare offices after many discussions. This is important because there is a lot of expertise and a lot of know-how in the ombuds offices, but often the framework conditions are not so good and the scope for action is far too small. That's where the novelle will make a significant improvement. But with this package, of course, our work is not done, but on the contrary: In autumn we will present a second animal welfare package, which concerns improvements in home and wildlife farming. This includes the prohibition of qualifications and the obligation to practice in exodus and wildlife farming. We will work intensively on this over the summer because it is not just agriculture that produces problems and animal suffering in Austria. Today's animal welfare package is an important success in the sense of animal welfare. We have received this feedback several times over the last few days from the animal protection NGOs and the animal rights activists. That is why today is a particularly motivating day for the further construction sites that are still in front of us. – Thank you to all those who support us on this path.

Animal welfare: What are we talking about? – We talk about the fate of millions of cattle, calves, pigs, chickens and many other so-called farm animals, which are largely excluded from our human compassion. At least this has been the case so far, because now there are innovations which can certainly hope for better times in the field of animal welfare, for example a minister who at least regularly expresses himself on the subject of animal welfare. Well, I think that has very diverse reasons, but one is sure that consumers don't care about how the animals are doing. Even though many of them cannot imagine a life as vegetarians or vegans, the life of an animal has been given a different priority. That is why it has long been time for political first-time measures to be taken, but also because the industry – despite inflation and the associated inflation – otherwise saws a little on the branch on which it sits, because we need to highlight and promote special features in times of global competition. Otherwise one day only mass animal farming remains, and then there is no need for normal farms, but only huge, ethically more than worrying animal factories, where it is enough to exploit auxiliary workers or temporary workers and not to put any value on animal welfare. The mass will then only return, and animal welfare will become an exclusion criterion in view of the world market. If there are no more excuses, then we will stick to the economic arguments. There are jobs in agriculture and meat processing, and Austria, its farmers and its agriculture, as well as the animals, deserve a comprehensive, well-appointed approach to a worry-free future. One thing is quite clear: Mass animal husbandry, as it is currently being forced, does not promote the peasantry, does not promote the traditions, but destroys them, including the numerous cultural landscapes. Animal welfare is therefore more than modern – also considered from the economic point of view. In this package there are some serious gaps, and there is much, much potential for improvement. A lot of things have already been said by my previous speakers, but this package of measures is a small success because it has done a little bit, but it has just broken the ice. However, I am concerned that there will be a long standstill on the subject of animal protection and that nothing will be packed and implemented again, because a first modern small building block was laid. Weak is definitely the solution for the transport of calves. At about three weeks, they are not only too weak for transport, they can also not handle the trip without being supplied according to age. The drinks are actually superfluous, as the calves still have another drinking reflex, and I do not mention now the standars, the darkness, because often the path to slaughter is the only way that these calves ever see daylight. Many diseases also arise on this journey. But why should we talk about these cruel issues on the subject of animal welfare? The solution with regard to full-column floors is also rather poor, because unfortunately I do not have the impression that something was thought through. Animal welfare is a constant discussion of the needs, sensations and fates of the farm animals. Looking for solutions that just give you a few more years of rest, because a little bit has happened anyway, is too little. In connection with animal protection, let us talk about the moral accountability of consumers! In connection with mass animal husbandry, let us talk about the greed for profit behind it and an interpretation of the animal welfare law, which, on the one hand, considers all these crimes to be correct! Fiaker – and I am grateful for this request, Mr Federal Minister, you once mentioned it – is also an important issue. I am often addressed in discussions that horses are steppe animals, that they also stand on pasture all day in the bright sun and are totally heat-sensitive. I've never, never seen a horse on the pasture that has to pull a five-person carriage in the heat. By the way, only once: A horse weighing 500 kg must drink 50 to 80 litres every day when working hard. I don't have the impression that this is perfectly guaranteed at the pitches. Finally, you can say: Yes, it is an animal welfare package, even if only the numerous dedicated animal protectionists in Austria would rightly have wanted more and I would have expected something that was called milestone in animal welfare. Thank you.

Year 2020: A new virus has hit an immunologically totally naïve population – that is, unprotected – and accordingly the virus has hit, not only with us, but worldwide. At that time, 2 percent of the identified infected persons were found to have died. When even less tested, this percentage was even higher, and it was really a dramatic situation. There has been virtually no specific medical measures against it. That is to say, one had to confine oneself to so-called non-pharmacological interventions, which means keeping measures from distance to lockdown – measures that have really been very intensive in our economic and social life. This was essentially the development of 2020, and it succeeded in preventing a lot of bad, but with great effort and with great restrictions. In 2021, the vaccinations became available, and it was seen very quickly that these vaccinations can be very well protected from severe events and from death, and they have protected them from the infection at the beginning and over many months and can inhibit the infection as a whole. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Worm.]] This has been so beyond the middle of 2021. The alpha variant at the beginning of the year and the delta variant were added more in the second half of the year. This was the occasion for one to think about a vaccination law, but one has already taken into account that one cannot predict from a present state of knowledge and a course of the pandemic how it will look half a year later. That was the reason why this law on vaccination was written as a framework law, with the concrete effect of each being dependent on the current situation to be assessed and then regulated by regulation. In this year – third phase – Omikron has come: massively infectious, much milder procedures, much less stress on the hospitals, on the one hand because this type per se makes a little less severe diseases, but especially because we now have a broad basic immunity in the population through vaccination and recovery. As a result, the situation is different. In addition, medications are now also available, which can actually be used in the event of outbreak of the disease. These too are able to further reduce the risk of the disease. So the situation has become another one, and that is exactly why we can put the Enforcement Act out of force today. I think that's right. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e25080	Mr President, I should like to thank the President-in-Office of Mr. Minister of Finance! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! No democracy is perfect. Like any political system, it has its characteristics, certain things that are good, and others that, successfully said, are capable of expanding. What makes democracy unique as a system, however, is that it can change and improve itself from within. Most changes are small, even minimal, and they are unfortunately also very rare. From time to time, however, a reform that not only meets with broad support in the population but is also praised by experts and also finds a broad consensus within the house. I would like to expressly thank the SPÖ and NEOS for the very good cooperation in connection with this law, because, ladies and gentlemen, the party law that we are going to adopt here today in this House is one of these rare cases. Of course, such a thing is not easy. There has been much negotiation in recent months, both inside and outside the coalition. With the current improvements to the 2012 existing party law – in these ten years, little has changed in our policy and little has happened – a new era of transparency and democratic control in party funding begins. We have been fighting Greens in Austria for decades. We have seen again and again in recent years that healthy democracy can only remain healthy with a strong, capable and above all independent top financial control body. Unfortunately, the party law in its present form has not always made life easy for our Court of Auditors. So far, the Court has only received rudimentary information on party finances and election costs, no detailed breakdowns of election costs, no direct access to accounting and zero insight into advertising transactions. There was a much too high reporting limit for party donations and permissible fines in the event of infringements. In short, it was a law that did not make the veiling and beautifying of the party funds more difficult and with the help of which such crimes could not be punished sufficiently. Despite these obstacles, the last few years have shown what a decent Court of Auditors has done, and it is also pleasing that he can continue to do so under the firm leadership of President Margit Kraker. I am very glad that Margit Kraker will continue to carry out this task, because it does this really great, like the entire Court of Auditors. Thank you so much to the whole team. [[Congratulation with Greens and NEOS as well as with representatives of the ÖVP.]] It is clearer today than ever that there can only be healthy democracy policy with independent insight and examination by control bodies. In the future, the Court of Auditors will be able to do much more for confidence in policy in our country. In the future, the Court of Auditors will be able to exercise a much more comprehensive control function, to take into account party books, to inform the Constitutional Court accordingly in the event of inconsistencies, and to review the election expenses of all parties six months after a election. In addition, not only revenue and expenditure, but also assets, debt, loans and also loans will have to be disclosed in the future, and a wide-ranging obligation on media inserity will ensure the necessary transparency in election periods. A year-round donation transparency – Mr Scherak has already mentioned it – will be added: Party donations are subject to reporting as early as 500 euros and have to be given a name so that the collection of larger donations, which has always been the subject, is made much more difficult. This is another important point in order to obtain transparency. We will continue to strengthen our High House and our control rights as Parliament, because in future Parliament clubs will be able to order a special audit by the Court of Auditors alone, which is a major step in the democratic legitimacy of the Austrian Parliament. We also need transparency in the associations and the statutes. This is a very important point, without which it will not go. Therefore, I make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] I think that this is an important step, because these statutes are simply not publicly clear to us, and this is also a matter for us in the Committee of Inquiry. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e25529	Mr President. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Values Advisors! Let us think of these objections as being complete: In principle, all organisations defined as related are also subject to accountability. This means that they must be included in the audit, and the flows of payments between these related organisations and the parties are fully comprehensible. All the other organizations, i.e. all these associations that have been challenged here, are led as donors when they pay something to the party. That means they're all party donations. However, if they are not attributable to the party and donate, they are subject to the ceiling, the individual ceiling of 7 500 euros, and these donations are to be included in the overall limit. This means that even since these circumvention structures are largely impossible, donations are only possible up to these small amounts. That means that it is not possible to give millions to any association, so that it can pass it on to the party unverified, unverified, because there are precisely these limits of donations. ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e29444	The opinion is now delivered by Mr Tursky State Secretary. Please, Mr State Secretary.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e29465	Mr President. Honourable Members! The tax legislation has the employees, the self-employed and the enterprises. To relieve it, it should be as easy as possible to apply, the key to administrative simplification – to which I will also have a lot to contribute as the State Secretary of the State – and, increasingly important, it should also support climate-friendly behaviour, as we particularly see today. We have taken all of this into account in the present tax-amending law, and I would like to draw a few points out in particular. For companies there are, as mentioned above, tax reliefs in research funding. In principle, companies can claim a research premium for research expenditure. To date, companies' own research performance has not been included in the basis for the calculation of this premium. This is supposed to change now, and this is also particularly important for the start-up scene in our country, because especially in start-ups and small companies, the management is usually also significantly involved in research and development work. Another major improvement: One application usually includes several research projects. So far, a uniform decision has been taken on an application. In disputed cases, therefore, the payment of the premium for the indisputable part has been massively delayed. It will now be the case that the indisputable part of the application will immediately be disbursed. Important tax reliefs exist as mentioned above also for social security benefits. The aim is to prevent any accidental increase in the tax burden on service providers due to conflicts of payments. Furthermore, payments from collective social and continuing training funds are exempt from tax and social security for up to a certain amount. After the outbreak of the Corona pandemic, we have introduced a number of tax benefits and tax exemptions, which we now have to extend – Corona is unfortunately, as we see again in these days, not history and will accompany us even further. This concerns, for example, the exemption from tax on protective masks or the exemption from fees related to the Covid pandemic. Changes have already been made, and I should like to highlight this particularly positively, because we are just trying to create an incentive – there is also in the interaction between public tickets and penalty fees. Those who have received the air-conditioning ticket from the employer for the entire federal territory cannot also receive the shuttle fee in full – I think that must inform everyone. In order not to provide false incentives here, therefore, climate tickets and penalty fees are calculated against. A further relief concerns the self-employed: In order to give them an incentive to use public transport, they will in future be able to reduce 50 percent of their costs for tickets on a flat-rate basis as operating expenditure. In order to further promote renewable energy, private income from the supply of photovoltaic electricity up to a certain amount of energy should also be tax-free, as mentioned above. I would therefore ask you to give your assent. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e30127	We're now on the 28th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Magnificence comes to the fore. Martin Engelberg. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_2022-07-07-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00168_d8e30158	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Multilateral development cooperation in the field of international financial institutions is not just a core area of the international development cooperation, but also a key part of international efforts to implement the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development. This IFI contribution law 2022 is intended to continue to support people in developing countries. The aim is to improve the living conditions of the population in the recipient countries. At the same time, Austria is making an important contribution to international solidarity and also to financing climate protection projects. The institutions should also assist beneficiary countries in combating the health and food crisis caused by Covid-19 and in reducing the social and economic consequences of the crisis in the medium term. The purpose of the merger of these projects in a legislative proposal is to reduce the number of otherwise required legislative procedures and to simplify the administrative burden. The pooling of the resources of many donors is intended to provide multilateral development banks with an efficient and effective opportunity to support developing countries in a coordinated and coherent manner and to promote the achievement of the sustainable development goals, the SDGs, under Agenda 2030. Overall, the adjustments in the years 2022 to 2033 provide for more than 500 million euros for Austrian contributions. The IDA, the International Development Organisation, is granted a contribution of more than EUR 435 million by Austria, and the re-filling of the Global Environment Facility Trust Fund is an Austrian contribution of almost EUR 60 million. Contributions to the Austrian ODA quota – you know, in this respect, we have the goal of reaching 0.7 percent of GDP – are acceptable and partly also for international climate finance. All in all, this is an important contribution both to the implementation of the Paris climate agreement and to the achievement of the 0.7 percent ODA quota. These are significant sums for development cooperation. In addition to bilateral development cooperation, the support of multilateral EZA instruments is of great importance, as has already been said, with regard to current movements of flight and escape, so we are very much in favour of this proposal. Thank you.

I'll tell you a story about Markus Unger, my district fire brigade deputy. He was in an international exercise in Croatia, where there were a lot of fire brigades, and at one time the siren went off. They all walked into the place and saw what was going on. There the locals are standing and have looked, and our firemen have said: What's going on? Why doesn't the fire brigade come? Then the locals said: The next fire brigade is 50 kilometers away. – So much about overstructuredness. I mean, we can really be glad that we have such a dense network of fire brigades. [[Congratulations and bravado with the ÖVP as well as congratulation with Green MEPs.]] In the communities, the commitment of the fire brigade goes far beyond its own task. The comrades are there when it burns when an accident happens when storm disasters happen, and they support the rescue service. And, ladies and gentlemen, they organize festivals and help with other clubs to organize festivals – and that is a great contribution to our social life in the country. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Smart]] That's what you can do. You may not imagine, but we're glad we have the fire brigades. So you see: There's nothing going on without the fire brigades. That is why it is right and important to maintain the structures of fire brigades for as long as possible. Today I would like to close with a great deal of thanks to all the fire brigades. - Thank you.
